Son güncelleme (previous): 2026-08-08 (**F2-CT-32-R2 — ImagingRequest PATCH/Cancel Residual Race Remediation (phase F2 — Modular Boundaries and Public Contracts; branch `fix/f2-ct-32-r2-imaging-request-residual-race`, baseline `origin/main` @ `51e102d19a5ddda87a49ad0367172b3eaae05d09`; BLOCKER/URGENT, opened by a real post-merge main CI failure — `ci-main-and-nightly` run `31276844302`, Layer 3 disposable-PostgreSQL job, `imagingRequestConcurrencyCharacterization.test.ts` 148 passed/6 failed, round-1 signature `PATCH=200,cancel=200,final=cancelled`, aggregate `EXACTLY_ONE_WINNER=29/30`, `BOTH_SUCCESS_SILENT_CLOBBER=1/30`).** Independently reproduced deterministically, not accepted as flake: 0/600 natural local rounds against low-latency disposable Postgres (cancel structurally faster than PATCH every round, so it always wins the CAS race locally — matches the same local-vs-CI timing asymmetry already documented for `patientEmergencyContactsConcurrency.ts`'s F1-004-P1-R2-R3), but a new test-only forced-interleaving hook (`installImagingRequestRaceTestHooks` in `imaging.ts`, mirroring that file's own established `installEmergencyContactRaceTestHooks` pattern) forcing cancel's read to begin only after PATCH's compare-and-set write already committed reproduced the exact CI signature deterministically, on demand. **Root cause:** the merged F2-CT-32-R1 CAS guard is airtight for any writer whose own read is stale at write time (proven unregressed: `imagingRequestConcurrencyGuard.test.ts` re-verified **73/73**) — the gap is that CT-32 races a *chainable* pair (`scheduled` is itself a valid predecessor of `cancelled`), and when PATCH's whole operation completes before cancel's own read executes, cancel legitimately observes `scheduled` and legitimately commits `scheduled -> cancelled`; both responses are individually honest, the pair still violates the invariant. `imagingRequestConcurrencyGuard.test.ts`'s own header comment for scenario A had already named this exact chainable-pair ambiguity as something to design around. Matches, and is resolved by, this codebase's own already-accepted precedent (`patientEmergencyContactsConcurrency.ts`, F1-004-P1-R2-R3): once a competing transaction has already committed before this transaction's own first read executes, the two are, from PostgreSQL's own point of view, genuinely and unambiguously sequential — no signal inside the second transaction alone can distinguish that from a deliberate sequential call. **Fix:** additive, optional `expectedStatus` precondition field on both PATCH (existing body) and cancel (new optional body) — the caller's own pre-request belief, immune to server-side read timing by construction, same technique as that file's own `expectedCurrentPrimaryContactId` token-protected mode. Omitted, behavior is byte-for-byte F2-CT-32-R1 (including its now-documented, characterized-not-silently-widened residual gap for opt-out callers). **No schema/migration** — confirmed not needed. **Tests:** `typecheck` clean; CT-32 revised in place (its own established "revise, don't delete" convention) to supply `expectedStatus`, re-run at `CT32_ROUNDS=100`: **504/504**, `EXACTLY_ONE_WINNER` 100/100, clobber 0/100; new `imagingRequestConcurrencyForcedInterleaving.test.ts` **55/55** — proves, under the identical forced schedule, legacy mode still reproduces `BOTH_SUCCESS` every round (characterization) while the fix makes it impossible every round, plus sequential PATCH-then-cancel (E), sequential cancel-then-PATCH-rejected (F), two-row independence (G), cross-tenant unchanged (H); `test:imaging` **103/103**; `test:imaging-lifecycle-facade` **34/34**; `test:imaging-study-request-patient-consistency` **12/12**; `test:imaging-study-request-patient-mismatch-detector` **7/7**; `guardrail:test` **74/74**; `guardrail:scan` exit 0; `git diff --check` clean; full `npm run test:runtime:postgres` orchestrator run (the exact command CI Layer 3 runs) against disposable PostgreSQL 16 (`postgres:16-alpine`, digest-pinned) — `outcome.exitCode: 0`, `"tests passed"`, `"cleanup succeeded"`. Files changed: `server/src/routes/imaging.ts`, `server/src/schemas/index.ts`, `server/src/tests/imagingRequestConcurrencyCharacterization.test.ts` (revised), `server/src/tests/imagingRequestConcurrencyForcedInterleaving.test.ts` (new), `server/package.json` (script wiring), this file, `CURRENT_PHASE.md`, `phases/F2_MODULAR_BOUNDARIES.md`, `evidence/README.md`, new evidence file. Full evidence: [evidence/F2-CT-32-R2_IMAGING_REQUEST_RESIDUAL_RACE_REMEDIATION.md](evidence/F2-CT-32-R2_IMAGING_REQUEST_RESIDUAL_RACE_REMEDIATION.md). **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED`** — `PR_OPENED` once opened (this task does not merge) / `NOT_MERGED` / `NOT_DEPLOYED` / `NOT_PRODUCTION_VERIFIED`. **`F2-STAGE3-AUTH-001`'s merged authorization decision is NOT revoked.** **`F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001` remains explicitly BLOCKED** until this PR merges and exact post-merge `main` CI is independently re-verified green. **Exact next task:** open the PR against `main` (do not merge); await CI + program-controller review; only after merge + green post-merge CI may `F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001` resume.)

`Clinic`/`UserClinic` are owned by the route's own domain. **Four new public read contracts introduced, one per owning domain**, each a small purpose-built module (not a shared "dashboard service"): `server/src/services/appointments/organizationAppointmentMetrics.ts` (`clinical-appointments-availability`), `server/src/services/patientOrganizationMetrics.ts` (`clinical-patients`), own, and reproducing every `where`/`_sum` predicate and post-processing formula (the `noShowRate` rounding, `Number(...) || 0` coercions) unchanged from the pre-refactor handler. Previous update: 2026-08-07 (**F2-GUARDRAIL-VAL-004-R1 — Address External Architecture Review Findings on PR #332 (phase F2 — Modular Monolith Boundary Remediation / Guardrail Signal Quality; branch `docs/f2-guardrail-val-004-fresh-signal-quality`, PR #332, not merged).** Resolves the two blocking findings from an external architecture review of PR #332 (reviewed head `e8e57bd8bb6ce7e3dd950a808cf4f1d31c1a8628`, this task's own starting head): **(1) high-risk sampling partition was asymmetric** — `highRiskCategories(e)`/`isHighRisk(e)` correctly flag an edge high-risk when either `ownerDomain` or `callerDomain` is a high-risk domain, but `buildVal004Sample.mjs`'s census/oversample allocation was keyed only by `ownerDomain`, so caller-side-only high-risk edges could be sampled under an ordinary standard-proportional stratum. **Fixed** with a new deterministic, mutually-exclusive `highRiskDomainKey(e)` (owner domain if itself high-risk, else caller domain) driving the partition, plus a fix-adjacent double-counting defect (standard-proportional population/selection re-querying the full remaining pool without excluding already-high-risk-routed edges) caught and fixed in the same pass — the corrected partition was verified to sum to exactly N=552 with 0 unassigned/duplicated edges. **(2) the 21.10% figure was mislabeled as a formal `conservative violation-rate ceiling`** despite having no simultaneous/global confidence-coverage guarantee (non-zero-event strata keep an unbounded plug-in rate; ambiguous edges are complete-case-excluded; a sum of independent marginal one-sided bounds carries no stated joint coverage). **Fixed via the review's own preferred minimal-relabel path:** `conservativeWeightedUpperBound()` renamed to `zeroEventSensitivityAnalysis()`, `p_hat_conservative_violation_rate_ceiling`/`p_hat_conservative_accepted_rate` renamed to `sensitivityViolationRate`/`sensitivityAcceptedRate` plus new `hasConfidenceCoverage: false` and an explicit `confidenceCoverageNote` field; computation unchanged in substance, language and field names now make the no-coverage-guarantee status explicit and machine-readable. **Consequence:** the corrected partition changed the actual reviewed sample from 192 to **266 edges** (159 reused with prior classification carried over unchanged, 33 not re-selected, 107 newly and independently classified against current source by 4 parallel sub-agents — 0 new true positives, 0 new ambiguous, 0 new scanner/domain-map defects found among them); headline weighted-accepted-rate moved 99.27% → **99.64%**; the sensitivity value moved 21.10% → **28.02%** (more non-census zero-event strata are now covered, not because uncertainty increased). Regenerated deterministically (sample manifest and metrics each re-run 2×+, byte-identical); 6 new regression tests added (`__tests__/val004Determinism.test.js`, now 50/50 passing) directly covering the caller-only/owner-only/both-sides high-risk partition cases, exact-N accounting, rerun determinism, and the sensitivity-analysis relabeling. Full detail: [evidence/F2-GUARDRAIL-VAL-004_FRESH_STRATIFIED_SIGNAL_QUALITY.md §0.1](evidence/F2-GUARDRAIL-VAL-004_FRESH_STRATIFIED_SIGNAL_QUALITY.md). Scope unchanged: `docs/program/**`, `docs/program/evidence/**`, reproducible validation scripts under `scripts/architecture-guardrail-validation/` only — no scanner, domain-map, accepted-baseline, runtime, Prisma/migration, auth, tenancy, or CI-enforcement code touched. **`BLOCKING_ENFORCEMENT = NOT_AUTHORIZED`, unchanged.** **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED`** — `TESTS_PASSED` (locally) / `PR_OPENED` (already open, PR #332) — `CI_PASSED`/`MERGED`/`DEPLOYED`/`PRODUCTION_VERIFIED` not claimed, pending independent verification. **Exact next task:** external re-review of this correction round; still not merged; do not merge without that re-review.)

Previous update: 2026-08-05 (**F1-004-P1-R2-R3 — Emergency Contact CREATE-Race Residual Limitation Closed via Optimistic-Concurrency Precondition Token (phase F1 — Engineering Safety Net and Concurrency Verification; same branch/PR as F1-004-P1-R2 below, follow-up task, does not touch or block the F2-GUARDRAIL/F2-SEC thread).** Same worktree/branch (`fix/f1-004-p1-r2-emergency-contact-update-race`, PR #325), starting from that task's own head `85f7c52c5a63ff40d21c8261dbebe685138cdfbf`; baseline re-fetched `origin/main` @ `32adf919cb331ae2ce4f681bd37e857ded8065ab`. **Objective:** prove the exact root cause of the CREATE-race CI failure R2's own §15 recorded but did not resolve (run [31020654709](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/actions/runs/31020654709) attempt 1, `A=201 B=201` on a fresh patient's first contact), and close it with a database-backed fix guaranteeing exactly one 201 + one 409 for genuinely overlapping CREATE requests — not accepted as a residual limitation without proving every narrower design impossible. **Root cause, deterministically reproduced (not probabilistic) via real, production-embedded test-only synchronization hooks** (`installEmergencyContactRaceTestHooks`, no-op unless a test installs them) forcing request B's entire promoting critical section to begin only after request A's transaction had fully committed: R2's "prior vs current" comparison, even correctly co-located on one connection, cannot detect a race where the LOSING request's own first read (not just its lock wait) is delayed past the winner's commit — at that point both reads observe identical, fully-committed state, which is, from PostgreSQL's own point of view, formally indistinguishable from a deliberate, later, sequential replacement. 500 local rounds (no forcing) reproduced this zero times, confirming the CI-observed rate depends on real network/scheduling jitter absent on a low-latency local box — matching why prior local runs never caught it. **Impossibility proof:** demonstrated by elimination that no further purely-server-side signal (reordering lock/read order, a per-patient `SELECT...FOR UPDATE` sentinel row, SERIALIZABLE isolation — re-confirmed still invalid per R2's own cross-tenant false-positive finding, or an app-server wall-clock heuristic — rejected as introducing unprovable clock-skew dependence, not merely as "more work") can airtight-ly distinguish "genuinely HTTP-concurrent but DB-serialized-by-scheduling" from "genuinely sequential" once a transaction holds the per-patient advisory lock — the database has no record of when the HTTP request arrived, only of commit order. Escalated per this task's own stop condition ("meeting the 201/409 contract requires a public API semantic change") rather than decided unilaterally; **program owner selected: an additive, optional, backward-compatible `expectedCurrentPrimaryContactId` precondition field** (omitted = legacy best-effort fallback, unchanged and knowingly not race-free; explicit `null` = client observed no primary; a contact id = client observed that exact primary) on both `POST`/`PUT`, enforced under the existing per-patient advisory lock as a TRUE optimistic-concurrency check against the client's own pre-formed belief — airtight regardless of connection-pool/event-loop scheduling, since it never depends on this request's own read timing relative to a competitor's commit. Verified airtight under the exact worst-case forced full-serialization schedule that broke every prior design: 20/20 forced deterministic rounds, then 500/500 token-protected CREATE stress rounds and 500/500 token-protected UPDATE stress rounds (natural races) — all correct, zero exceptions. Legacy (no-token) behavior is unchanged and its known limitation is preserved via a permanent characterization test that asserts it still reproduces under the same forced interleaving — never silently hidden. **Frontend updated:** `PatientEmergencyContactsPanel.tsx`/`PatientEmergencyContactForm.tsx` now always send the precondition (including explicit `null`) for every create/replace-primary operation, sourced from the panel's own last-loaded contact list. **No migration** — `server/prisma/schema.prisma` and `migrations/` untouched (`git diff --stat -- server/prisma/` empty); the existing partial unique index remains the unchanged backstop. **Tests:** `patientEmergencyContacts.test.ts` 31/31 (+6, `parseExpectedPrimaryPrecondition`); new `patientEmergencyContactsCreateRaceForcedInterleaving.test.ts` 7/7 (deterministic forced-interleaving proof + 5 precondition-mismatch scenarios: null-vs-UUID, UUID-vs-different-UUID, stale frontend state, sequential replacement preserved, cross-tenant isolation); `patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts` 19/19 (legacy 100/150 unchanged + new token-protected 500/500 CREATE and 500/500 UPDATE stress); server typecheck clean; frontend typecheck clean; 40/40 frontend component tests (+4 new); full `test:runtime:postgres` disposable-DB profile run **5 independent successful process-level times**, zero residual Docker resources each time. Files changed: `server/src/routes/patientEmergencyContacts.ts`, `server/src/services/patientEmergencyContacts.ts`, `server/src/services/patientEmergencyContactsConcurrency.ts` (production); `server/src/tests/dbVerification/patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts`, `patientEmergencyContactsCreateRaceForcedInterleaving.test.ts` (new), `server/src/tests/patientEmergencyContacts.test.ts` (tests); `src/components/PatientEmergencyContactForm.tsx`, `PatientEmergencyContactsPanel.tsx` + their vitest suites (frontend); `server/package.json` (new npm script); this file, `CURRENT_PHASE.md`, `phases/F1_CI_AND_TEST_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `evidence/README.md`, evidence file (documentation). **PR [#325](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/pull/325) remains open, `CLEAN`/`MERGEABLE`, 0 unresolved review threads — not merged, not deployed, not production-verified.** This is an additive API-surface change (backward-compatible, but still an API change), so per this task's own instructions it is reported for review, not merged unilaterally. **Final CI on this task's own head `2ebfc2c9eb086116c123939b82ae4df452b84a8f`: run [31031917887](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/actions/runs/31031917887), `success`, 10/10 jobs green.** **F1-004-P1-R2-R4** (2026-08-05, same PR, documentation-only metadata/program-status reconciliation, no runtime/frontend/test/schema/migration/workflow file touched) corrected this entry's own stale `CI run pending on push` wording and updated the PR title/body to match. **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED` / `PR_OPENED` / `PR_CI_PASSED` / `REVIEW_THREADS_RESOLVED` — `NOT_MERGED` / `NOT_DEPLOYED` / `NOT_PRODUCTION_VERIFIED`, merge-ready after R4's own docs-only CI passes.** Full evidence: [evidence/F1-004-P1-R2_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_UPDATE_RACE_ROOT_CAUSE_AND_FIX.md §16–25](evidence/F1-004-P1-R2_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_UPDATE_RACE_ROOT_CAUSE_AND_FIX.md). **Exact next task:** external review and merge decision for PR #325; Prior update: 2026-08-05 (**F1-004-P1-R2 — Emergency Contact Update Race: Root-Cause Confirmation and Fix (phase F1 — Engineering Safety Net and Concurrency Verification; independent of, does not touch or block, the F2-GUARDRAIL/F2-SEC thread below).** Fresh isolated worktree `D:\Mustafa\Siteler\DisKlinikCRM-worktrees\f1-004-p1-r2-emergency-contact-update-race`, branch `fix/f1-004-p1-r2-emergency-contact-update-race`, baseline freshly-fetched `origin/main` @ `9b10bc9fde0ceaeb58d90860a950143de7123910` (PR #323's own merge commit) — confirmed clean at task start; did not reuse the pre-existing unmerged `fix/f1-004-p1-emergency-contact-primary-update-concurrency` (PR #311, closed unmerged as redundant) or `hotfix/us-01-2-primary-update-race` (PR #310, already merged) worktrees. **Parent context, independently re-verified:** main CI run [31002888303](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/actions/runs/31002888303) attempt 1 (job `92295979999`) failed `patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts`'s concurrent-UPDATE scenario with `A=200, B=200`, on a commit that **already included** PR #310's advisory-lock + optimistic-recheck fix (merged 2026-08-03, closing #309); attempt 2 (job `92298209814`) of the same run passed with no code change — not dismissed as flaky. **Root cause, deterministically reproduced (not a correlation):** PR #310's "prior primary" optimistic-concurrency snapshot was read via a SEPARATE, unlocked Prisma query issued before its promoting transaction opened — an event independently racing the transaction's own connection checkout against the shared 10-connection pg pool (`server/src/db.ts`). Under contention, the losing request's prior-read could be delayed past the winning request's full commit, silently absorbing the winner's row as "prior truth," so the in-lock recheck found no discrepancy. A temporary, non-committed forced-interleaving harness (`AsyncLocalStorage`-gated monkey-patch, deleted before this PR — never merged) reproduced `A=200, B=200` on demand against the pre-fix code; the database's own single-primary invariant was **never** violated (final state always exactly one committed primary) — only the "loser must get 409" HTTP contract was. **Alternative considered and rejected:** SERIALIZABLE isolation (read-before-lock, relying on Postgres's own predicate-lock conflict detection instead of a manual comparison). A raw-SQL probe (no Prisma, no app code) confirmed it correctly aborts genuine overlapping races (SQLSTATE `40001`) — but re-running the full concurrency suite under this design broke the suite's own tenant-isolation scenario (two unrelated patients, different organizations, must never interfere): on this small/sparse table, Postgres's planner favors a sequential scan for the "current primary" read, so SERIALIZABLE's predicate locks land at page/table granularity, producing false-positive `409`s between completely unrelated patients — an unacceptable cross-tenant regression, rejected before being proposed as the fix. **Fix implemented:** both the "prior" and "current" reads around the existing per-patient `pg_advisory_xact_lock` now run on the SAME `prisma.$transaction` (same connection) as each other — the only gap between them is the lock-wait itself, never a second independently-poolable database round trip. Default (READ COMMITTED) isolation, unchanged. **No migration** — the existing partial unique index (`PatientEmergencyContact_one_primary_per_patient`) is unchanged and remains the backstop. **R2 review-remediation update (2026-08-05, same PR, commit `b01ea47124584f918351afd6feb02e6f46e4839a`, addresses 2/2 PR #325 review threads, now resolved):** `isPrimaryContactConflict()` no longer maps a generic Prisma `P2034` (deadlock/serialization failure) to `PRIMARY_CONTACT_CONFLICT` — it now falls through to the route's existing generic 500 handling; `PrimaryContactConflictError`/`P2002` classification unchanged; also added strictly-validated `PATIENT_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_{CREATE,UPDATE}_RACE_ROUNDS` local-diagnostic-only env overrides (CI never sets them, so CI always runs the full committed 100/150 defaults). **Tests (current, post-R2):** `patientEmergencyContacts.test.ts` 25/25 (net +1 vs. R1's 24/24 — removed the now-incorrect P2034-is-a-conflict test, added 2 covering the corrected non-classification); `patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts` 17/17 (R1's 8 concurrency/stress scenarios + 9 new stress-round-configuration-validation unit tests) — CREATE-race stress 100/100, UPDATE-race stress 150/150, same-contact-idempotent-update scenario, all unchanged; server typecheck clean; root `test:runtime:unit` 74/74; full `test:runtime:postgres` disposable-DB profile (all 16 chained scripts) run **3 independent successful process-level times against this exact post-R2 commit**, exit `0` each, zero residual Docker resources each time. Tenant/clinic scope re-verified unchanged: lock keyed by `patientId` alone; reset queries scoped to `(patientId, clinicId, organizationId)`; lock only acquired for `isPrimary=true` writes; no route-level authorization code touched. **Residual limitation, documented honestly, not glossed over:** no purely server-side mechanism (this fix, or the rejected SERIALIZABLE alternative) can formally guarantee a conflict signal when a request's ENTIRE database interaction happens to be scheduled strictly after a competing request's full commit — closing that would require a client-supplied concurrency token (changes API semantics, a task stop condition) or a global lock (explicitly forbidden); this fix closes the specific, proven mechanism behind the observed CI failure and empirically eliminates it across 250 real stress rounds over 3 independent runs. Full evidence: [evidence/F1-004-P1-R2_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_UPDATE_RACE_ROOT_CAUSE_AND_FIX.md](evidence/F1-004-P1-R2_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_UPDATE_RACE_ROOT_CAUSE_AND_FIX.md) + [.json companion](evidence/F1-004-P1-R2_emergency_contact_update_race_root_cause_and_fix.json). Files changed: `server/src/routes/patientEmergencyContacts.ts`, `server/src/services/patientEmergencyContacts.ts`, `server/src/services/patientEmergencyContactsConcurrency.ts` (production code); `server/src/tests/dbVerification/patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts`, `server/src/tests/patientEmergencyContacts.test.ts` (tests); `phases/F1_CI_AND_TEST_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CURRENT_PHASE.md`, `evidence/README.md`, this file, and the two new evidence files (documentation). No F2-guardrail/F2-SEC validation change included, per this task's own "one capability/risk per PR" instruction. **PR [#325](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/pull/325)**, reconciled against `origin/main` (post-PR #324) in this same task, commit `fd1148f8341311baee3db1b2a7313522d7ad2404` — **not merged, not deployed, not production-verified.** **CI on the reconciled head, first real-world observation of the documented residual limitation (evidence §15/JSON `firstRealCiObservationOfResidualLimitation`):** first attempt (run [31020654709](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/actions/runs/31020654709)) FAILED — `Layer 3: disposable PostgreSQL tests`, CREATE-race stress round 0, `A=201 B=201` (both concurrent creates for a brand-new patient's first contact succeeded) instead of one `201`+one `409` — matching the residual gap this fix's own evidence already names as theoretically unclosable, never before observed in real CI; 0/300 local reproductions across 3 independent disposable-DB runs on this exact commit. Diagnostic rerun of only the failed job (no code/test change) passed; overall run `success`. **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED` / `PR_OPENED_AWAITING_REVIEW`, CI green on rerun, program-owner review of the residual-limitation observation recommended before merge.** Merge safety: code change is narrowly scoped, all tests pass, no migration, no schema change — the open question is the acceptable real-world frequency of the residual race just observed, a program-owner decision. Prior update: 2026-08-03 (**F2-IMPL-001-A-R3 — Atomic Legal-Hold Enforcement and Final CI Reconciliation (continued on the existing open PR #304 branch `feature/f2-impl-001-a-unused-imaging-lifecycle-facade`; prior reviewed head `7bcd1c8658da95119ab725f454955d57f89f8e4f`, new head `cd29d0f...`; no new branch, no new PR).** **Pre-edit gate:** `origin/main` unchanged at `6f539b237019945443afe6156f9fc2a9fe32ffa4` (not advanced — no reconciliation required); `gh pr view 304` confirmed `OPEN`, head `9fdfb91739464809d442a9b8d14fa236adfaf624` (matched local/origin exactly, no divergence), `mergeable: MERGEABLE`; review threads re-checked — 6 comments total, all 3 original findings already replied-to/resolved in R2, zero new threads. **Blocking defect:** `redactForAnonymization` read `ImagingStudy.legalHold` and checked it in memory, but its `updateMany` write predicate never included `legalHold` — a hold acquired after the read but before the write had no effect on the write (TOCTOU race), letting a now-held image be silently redacted. **Fix:** the mutation predicate now is `{ id: imageId, clinicId, study: { clinicId, legalHold: false } }` — the check and the write are now the same database statement, closing the gap. **Zero-row classification** uses only a second, fully tenant-scoped `findOwnedImage` follow-up (never an unscoped-by-id lookup): not found under this tenant → `ImagingNotFoundError`; already redacted by a concurrent writer → benign no-op; otherwise (row exists, this tenant, not yet redacted) → `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError`, since of the four predicate fields only `study.legalHold` can independently change between read and write. **No cross-tenant leakage:** a cross-tenant `imageId` is rejected by the initial ownership read before the legal-hold branch is ever reached, so it can never surface `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError` — directly tested. **Idempotency/legal-hold precedence:** re-read F2-PREP-009 — it does not specify this ordering; this task's chosen (pre-existing, re-verified correct) behavior keeps the read-time legal-hold check ahead of the already-redacted short-circuit, so an already-redacted-but-now-held row still throws `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError` rather than silently no-op-succeeding. **Deterministic concurrency regression test** added (`server/src/tests/imagingLifecycleFacade.test.ts` §8b): a new test-only synchronization seam (`__setRedactionPreMutationBarrierForTest`, a bare `() => Promise<void>` with no `clinicId`/`imageId` access, so it cannot itself alter authorization behavior) pauses an in-flight `redactForAnonymization` call immediately before its `updateMany`, via promise-based gating (`await barrierReached`), never a fixed-duration sleep; legal hold is then acquired concurrently and the gate released — asserts `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError`, `originalName` unchanged, and an unrelated cross-tenant row untouched. Plus 3 more direct tests: a predicate-capture test proving `study.legalHold: false` is actually in the `updateMany` call's `where` clause (not only the in-memory check), a cross-tenant-held-image test (must be `ImagingNotFoundError`, never `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError`), and a denormalized-clinic-mismatch-under-legal-hold test. **Test seam review:** both `__setImagingStorageExistenceCheckerForTest` (pre-existing) and the new barrier seam retain Option B (module-private variable + narrow test-only setter) with full, precisely-scoped evidence written directly into `public.ts`'s doc comments — sequential single-process test execution, process-local state, every override `finally`-restored, zero production importers, cannot alter tenant predicates, and an explicit statement that this claim is scoped to sequential execution only, not general parallel-test safety. No parameter added to any accepted method. **Validation:** server `npx prisma generate && tsc --noEmit` exit `0`; `git diff --check` clean; `Imaging-Lifecycle-Facade` suite **34/34 passing** (was 30; +4 net, exactly the new §8b tests, zero regressions) on a real disposable PostgreSQL (`npm run test:runtime:postgres`, run `20260803T170803Z-37686cc0-46840`) — full `server:test:disposable-db` aggregate exit `0`, `cleanup.success: true`, zero residual Docker resources; `test:imaging-characterization`'s `CT-32` sub-suite independently re-confirmed **153/153**; `test:imaging` (direct, no disposable DB) **103/103**; `test:patient-privacy` (direct, no disposable DB) **38/38**; zero production callers re-confirmed for both the module and the new test seam (`grep`, excluding `*.test.ts`, zero matches outside the definition site). No `server/prisma/schema.prisma`, migration, route, or `.github/workflows/*.yml` file touched. **Windows Bridge CI:** the `Windows Bridge .NET tests` job (`BridgeOrchestratorTests.Heartbeat_AfterPairing_TransitionsToOnlineAndOmitsNullCapabilities`, `Assert.Contains` against an empty captured string) failed on this task's own fresh push (`cd29d0f`, run `30835968634`, attempt 1) — the identical failure signature seen on the prior R2 push (§27.16); zero `windows-bridge/` files touched by this PR at any point in its history; the `windows-bridge-pr` workflow triggers only on PR events and never runs against `main`, so no comparable control run exists. Classified **`FLAKY_UNVERIFIED`** (not `PR_CAUSED` — zero file overlap; not confirmable as `PRE_EXISTING_REPRODUCIBLE_ON_MAIN` — no control run obtainable; the defining flaky signature — identical commit, rerun flips the result — was independently confirmed on the R2 push, and reconfirmed on this push: `gh run rerun --failed` → passed). A rerun was triggered (`gh run rerun --failed`, mirroring established practice, not a code change) — final result recorded in this task's own delivery report and PR #304's final CI state; this document is not silently re-edited for status that becomes known after this line was written — any correction is its own new, additive entry. **Task status, CI-conditional:** if the final head's required checks are all green, `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED` / `REVIEW_THREADS_RESOLVED` / `PR_CI_PASSED` / `PR_OPENED_AWAITING_PROGRAM_REVIEW`; otherwise `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TARGETED_TESTS_PASSED` / `CI_LAYERS_PASSED` / `PR_CHECKS_FAILED_UNRELATED_BLOCKER` / `PR_OPENED_AWAITING_PROGRAM_REVIEW` — see the delivery report for which applies. Son güncelleme: 2026-08-03 (**F2-IMPL-001-A STATUS CORRECTION — Blocking Tenant-Authorization Finding and Signature-Drift Fix (correction/reclassification, continued on the existing open PR #304 branch `feature/f2-impl-001-a-unused-imaging-lifecycle-facade` — no new branch, no new PR opened for this correction itself, PR #304 not merged, not closed, left open and marked blocked).** **Two blocking findings confirmed by a subsequent architectural review of the F2-IMPL-001-A implementation recorded below (Prior update):** (1) **tenant authorization is not enforced.** The facade's `findOwnedImage()` re-derivation only checks that `ImagingImage.clinicId` agrees with its own `ImagingStudy.clinicId` — a data-integrity consistency check, not caller tenant authorization; none of `markStorageMissing(imageId)`/`redactForAnonymization(imageId, reason)`/`checkImageStorageExists(imageId)`'s accepted signatures carry any caller-supplied `clinicId`/principal/context to check the resolved value against, and no ambient tenant-context mechanism exists anywhere in this codebase. A caller holding a valid `imageId` belonging to another clinic can read/mutate it today as long as the row is internally consistent, which it always is for a non-corrupted row. (2) **`checkImageStorageExists` shipped with signature drift**: the accepted contract is `checkImageStorageExists(imageId: string): Promise<boolean>`; the implementation added an optional trailing `fileExistsForTest` parameter — a public contract change regardless of being optional/defaulted/test-only. **Mandatory actions taken, per instruction not to paper over finding 1 inside the current accepted contract:** implementation expansion stopped (no `clinicId`/context parameter added, no ambient-context mechanism invented, no caller wired to the facade); Prior update: 2026-08-02 (**F1-003-B2-R1 — Admission-Point Lock Closure, Current-Main Reconciliation, Remote CI Verification, and Evidence Sanitization (continued on the existing open branch `fix/f1-003-b2-retention-run-lock-race` / PR #280 — no new branch, no new PR, not merged, not rebased, not force-pushed).** **Main reconciliation:** fetched `origin/main` (`e8e05f669f049c4895e0c9a7f95db6f3fbd463fe`, PR #277 merge commit), confirmed both PR #276 (`0de9a04a...`) and PR #277 (`e8e05f66...`) merge commits are ancestors of `origin/main`, `git merge --no-ff origin/main` — **zero conflicts**, only 4 new additive F2-PREP-001/003 evidence files introduced, no schema/migration change; merge commit `2fc2d9b1985a33125d14251bca6077c149f589c4`; post-merge ancestor check confirmed true. **Residual race closed:** F1-003-B2's own evidence explicitly documented one remaining gap — the live-run route still read `getPlatformSetting` (policy/runtime-toggle) *before* `acquireJobLock`, so a sufficiently delayed second concurrent request could in principle still reach admission after the first had already released it. This task moves lock acquisition ahead of the policy read entirely, making admission the very first variable-latency DB operation a live attempt performs; both the winner and the lock-contention loser still read policy afterward, purely for audit-metadata accuracy, never as part of admission; a policy-gate rejection now releases the briefly-held lock and preserves the exact pre-existing `403`/audit shape; a policy-lookup failure after acquisition releases the lock and fails safely with a sanitized `500`, no fabricated audit row. **Route refactored into an exported factory** (`createDataRetentionRunHandler`, `server/src/routes/platformAdmin.ts`) so tests can inject a controllable `getPlatformSetting`; production always uses the real one. `releaseJobLock` (`jobLock.ts`) now logs — previously fully silent — when its own underlying DB release update genuinely fails; documented/tested as a deliberate trade-off (never rejects; lock honestly left held until TTL expiry, not silently cleared). **Deterministic concurrency proof (not timing-luck):** a new test fires two concurrent live requests through a `getPlatformSetting` override that signals via a controllable `Deferred` the instant it is entered (proving admission already resolved) then blocks on another `Deferred` (never a `setTimeout`) until the test releases it; while both racers are parked mid-policy-lookup, the test queries the `JobLock` row directly and confirms exactly one is held — admission fully decided before either policy lookup resolves — then releases both barriers and confirms the result is still exactly `[200, 409]`. **4 new tests** (deterministic admission proof; policy disabled after lock acquisition; policy lookup throws after lock acquisition; `releaseJobLock()` genuine-DB-failure trade-off) — `retentionManualRunAudit.test.ts` **25→29 assertions**, run **6 consecutive times, 29/29 every time**. Also passing: `npm run typecheck` (0 errors), `npm run test:data-retention` (42/42), `npm run server:test:non-disposable` (exit `0`, 0 failure markers), root `npm run test:runtime:postgres` (exit `0`, cleanup succeeded, zero residual `com.noramedi.test-runtime=true` Docker resources), `git diff --check` clean. **Remote CI: pushed to the existing branch, `gh pr checks 280` reports "no checks reported"; `statusCheckRollup` empty; the only observed run is an unrelated Copilot code-review bot, not a test workflow.** Diagnosed: `origin/main` has only `windows-bridge-pr.yml` (path-filtered away from this PR) and `windows-bridge-release.yml` (`workflow_dispatch`-only); the layered CI system that would run `test:retention-manual-run-audit` in CI exists only on PR #268 (F1-003-P3), not merged — a pre-existing structural gap this task cannot fix. **`REMOTE_CI_VERIFIED` is explicitly NOT claimed; all verification is `TEST_REPRODUCED` (local, real disposable PostgreSQL) or `CODE_PATH_CONFIRMED`.** **Evidence sanitized:** found and corrected exactly one author-machine absolute-path occurrence in task-owned files (`F1-003-B2_RETENTION_RUN_MUTUAL_EXCLUSION_RACE.md`'s "Branch/worktree" field), replaced with "fresh isolated worktree; local filesystem path intentionally omitted"; branch names/SHAs preserved; unrelated historical evidence elsewhere left untouched. Prior update: 2026-08-02 (**F1-003-B2 — Retention Manual/Scheduled Run Mutual-Exclusion Race (blocking production-behavior defect, discovered by F1-003-P3's Layer 5 compatibility CI on PR #268 — PR #268 left completely open, unmodified, and blocked by this task; fresh isolated worktree/branch `fix/f1-003-b2-retention-run-lock-race`, execution baseline `origin/main` @ `70b1690c1a656c95cead7b42812cc9ae6447bfb7`, not the F1-003-P3 worktree, zero intervening commits).** **Authoritative failure evidence:** PR #268 run `30542271302`, job `ci-layers / Layer 5: full-suite/compatibility fail-safe (backend, legacy server:test DB-required members)` — `server/src/tests/retentionManualRunAudit.test.ts`'s concurrent-manual-runs assertion failed with `actual=[200,200]` vs `expected=[200,409]` (`Toplam: 20 ✓ 19 ✗ 1`); the immediately-following scheduled/manual-collision test in the same run passed. **Root cause proved before any fix was written:** `server/src/utils/jobLock.ts`'s `acquireJobLock`/`releaseJobLock` primitive (shared by the manual route and the scheduled cron, identical lock key `'data-retention-cleanup'`, `JobLock.name` genuinely the Prisma `@id` primary key per its own migration SQL) is atomic and race-free — empirically stress-tested with 130 concurrent-acquisition trials against a real disposable PostgreSQL container using the exact production Prisma 7.8.0 + `@prisma/adapter-pg` driver-adapter stack, **0 double-grants**. The actual defect was structural ordering in `server/src/routes/platformAdmin.ts`'s `POST /privacy/data-retention/run`: the shared lock was acquired only *after* two other variable-latency DB round trips (`getPlatformSetting` policy-gate read, `recordManualRunStarted` durable audit-write transaction) that both racers perform independently; since live-run cleanup against a small dataset completes in single-digit milliseconds, a fast racer's entire acquire→cleanup→release cycle could finish before a simultaneously-`Promise.all`-fired second racer even reached its own lock attempt, letting both legitimately (sequentially, not concurrently) acquire the lock and both return `200` — never a flaw in the lock's own atomicity, a genuine application-contract violation, not a test-scheduling assumption. **Fix (smallest correction, no new locking subsystem, no schema/migration change):** `acquireJobLock`/`releaseJobLock` exported from `jobLock.ts` (previously private, `withJobLock` itself untouched and still used unchanged by the scheduled cron and every other job in the codebase); the manual route's live-run branch now acquires the lock **first** — before either audit write — with both the winner and the loser still writing their own `manual_run_started` row (the existing, already-accepted audit-shape contract unchanged), the loser branching immediately to `manual_run_blocked`/`concurrent_run_in_progress` + `409` with zero cleanup invoked; a new edge case (lock acquired, then the started-write itself fails) now releases the lock before returning `500`, preventing a newly-possible 2-hour TTL orphan; cleanup wrapped in `try/finally` releasing on success, throw, or any other exit. **Tests: 5 new + 1 strengthened in `retentionManualRunAudit.test.ts` (20→25 assertions)** — a 10-consecutive-round in-test race gate (real PostgreSQL, genuine `Promise.all` concurrency, not simulated), 3 new tests exercising `acquireJobLock`/`releaseJobLock` directly (4-way concurrent grant, release-then-reacquire, throw-then-reacquire), 1 new test for the lock-acquired-then-started-write-fails edge case, and the existing scheduled/manual-collision test strengthened with a seeded-row-untouched assertion. **Post-fix: `test:retention-manual-run-audit` run 8 consecutive times, 25/25 every time (80 total race-gate rounds embedded, 0 double-successes, 0 double-blocks) — combined with the isolated primitive stress test, 210+ total concurrent-acquisition trials, zero races.** Also passing: `npm run typecheck` (0 errors), `npm run test:data-retention` (42/42, unaffected — exercises the cleanup function via injected deps, not the route), `npm run server:test:non-disposable` (exit `0`, 0 failure markers), root `npm run test:runtime:postgres` (exit `0`, cleanup succeeded, zero residual `com.noramedi.test-runtime=true` Docker containers/networks after every run), `git diff --check` clean. **`server:test:legacy-db-required` does not exist on `origin/main`** (defined only inside PR #268's not-merged `ci-layers.yml`) — per instruction, the P3 CI aggregate was not copied onto this branch; Prior update: 2026-07-30 (**F1-003-P2-R3 — Parallel Signal Cleanup Registry and Interruption Verification (continued on the existing open PR #260 branch `feature/f1-003-p2-disposable-runtime` — no new PR opened, PR #260 not merged, not rebased, not force-pushed).** Fixes a defect in the orchestrator's SIGINT/SIGTERM best-effort cleanup path (distinct from F1-003-P2-R2's Prisma-generation race, below): a single module-scoped `let activeCleanupTargets` slot could represent only one invocation's cleanup state at a time, yet `verify-parallel`'s `Promise.all`-based postgres pair and storage pair each keep two invocations concurrently live in the same process as their normal, expected steady state — a signal during that window could see at most one invocation's containers/network, or `null`. **Fix:** new module `scripts/test-runtime/lib/cleanupRegistry.ts` — a `Map<runId, TeardownTargets>` registry storing each invocation's teardown targets **by reference** (in-place mutation as containers/networks are created is automatically visible, no separate "update" API needed); `registerCleanupTargets()`/`unregisterCleanupTargets()` replace the old slot; `teardownAllRegistered()` snapshots every registered invocation and tears each down via `Promise.allSettled` (never `Promise.all`, so one invocation's teardown failure cannot block the others); `runSignalCleanupOnce()` caches its in-flight promise so a second SIGINT/SIGTERM delivered mid-teardown reuses the same pass instead of starting an overlapping one. `lib/cleanup.ts` itself is unmodified. **9 new Docker-free unit tests** (concurrent registration; independent removal; snapshot covers post-registration mutation; one teardown failure does not block another; duplicate registration rejected deterministically; unknown/already-removed ID is harmless; a second signal mid-teardown reuses the same pass via a call-counter proof; registry empty after normal completion; test reset clears all state) — **68/68 total unit tests passing** (was 59/59). **Live interruption verification attempted, not claimed complete:** `child_process.spawn` + `child.kill('SIGINT')` empirically confirmed (isolated repro) to hard-kill the child on this Windows machine without invoking its own `SIGINT` handler; the stronger `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent(CTRL_C_EVENT, 0)` Win32 approach was attempted via PowerShell P/Invoke but not completed in this session (interactive approval not granted) — not claimed working or non-working. Falls back, per the task's own explicit instruction, to the deterministic unit-level multi-target signal-handler verification above; the orchestrator's real signal handlers call that exact unit-verified function unconditionally. **Reruns, all real Docker Desktop, this task:** `postgres`/`storage` profiles exit `0`; all 4 postgres failure-injection modes correct; `verify-parallel --inject-failure=parent-generate` still aborts before fan-out; `npm run server:test:non-disposable` exit `0` (68/68, no regression); `verify-parallel` run 3 consecutive times, all exit `0`, all `collisionCheck` `true`, zero labeled resources after the batch. One incidental finding, honestly recorded: an unrelated shell command-timeout hard-killed one standalone run mid-execution during this task's own regression testing, leaving one labeled container/network behind — the already-documented "not guaranteed to survive a hard kill" limitation, not a registry defect; removed manually as hygiene. **Optional supply-chain cleanup applied:** cleanup-failure-injection sidecar's `alpine:latest` replaced with the exact digest already cached locally, `alpine@sha256:28bd5fe8b56d1bd048e5babf5b10710ebe0bae67db86916198a6eec434943f8b`; no new pull; re-verified still reproducing the same genuine cleanup failure. Typecheck/PowerShell/bash/JSON/`git diff --check` all clean. Preserves unchanged: fail-fatal cleanup policy, per-run label ownership, stale-resource sweeper, production-endpoint guards, F1-003-P2-R2's parent-generate fix, digest-pinned images, existing failure-injection behavior. Prior update: 2026-07-30 (**F1-003-P2-R2 — Parallel Prisma Generation Race Elimination and Deterministic Runtime Verification (continued on the existing open PR #260 branch `feature/f1-003-p2-disposable-runtime` — no new PR opened, PR #260 not merged, not rebased, not force-pushed).** Resolves the EBUSY blocker F1-003-P2-R1 recorded below (its own `verify-parallel` reruns: run 1 success, **run 2 — one storage invocation failed with a real Windows EBUSY** during concurrent `prisma generate`, run 3 success). **Root cause** (CodeGraph confirmed unavailable, `ToolSearch` zero matches; bounded `Read`/`Grep` used instead, scoped to `scripts/test-runtime/**`, `server/prisma/schema.prisma`, `server/prisma.config.ts`, `server/src/db.ts`): `runMigrations()` ran `npx prisma generate` unconditionally on every disposable-profile invocation, targeting the shared, schema-derived default output `server/node_modules/.prisma/client`; concurrent `verify-parallel` children (2-way concurrency within the storage pair specifically — not full 4-way, since the postgres pair completes before the storage pair starts) raced on that shared directory. Generation output depends only on `schema.prisma`, never `DATABASE_URL`, so one generation safely serves every child in the same invocation; `migrate deploy` does not require generation first; no cross-process lock is needed since all concurrency is same-process. **Fix: Option A — generate once before parallel fan-out.** New module `scripts/test-runtime/lib/prismaGeneration.ts`: `generatePrismaClientOnce()` runs `prisma generate` exactly once per orchestrator invocation and mints a single-process authorization token (`node:crypto.randomBytes`); `runMigrations()` skips its own generate step only when presented a token this same process actually minted — any absent/foreign/invalid token falls back to generating (fail-safe, never fail-skip). Standalone `postgres`/`storage` invocations are byte-for-byte unaffected (no authorization is ever passed to them). If parent generation fails, `verify-parallel` aborts before any child/Docker resource is created. New test-only failure-injection mode `--inject-failure=parent-generate` verified this: `generation.succeeded: false`, zero Docker resources created, exit `1`. Acceptance-criteria items 17/18 (concurrent postgres/storage runs succeed without collision) corrected from "MET based on 2 runs, later found unproven" to "MET, re-verified via 5 consecutive clean runs post-fix" — the other 32 criteria unaffected. No schema/migration file created; existing migrations applied only to disposable databases during reruns; no production access; no `_prisma_migrations` change; no rollback performed. No lock/temp file of any kind is created by this mechanism (in-process token set only) — no stale-lock-file question applies. Prior update: 2026-07-20 (**KVKK-HIGH-008-F1 — second external-review correction pass (same branch `fix/kvkk-high008-runtime-toggle`, PR #186, MERGED merge commit 1a82302).** A second, independent external review of PR #186 found that the immediately-preceding correction pass's fix for the admin-toggle audit trail (entry below) was itself architecturally wrong: reusing `SecuritySignalEvent` — a security-detection telemetry store aggregated by `securityDetectionRules.ts` — for a platform-admin configuration change mixed two distinct domains (config-change audit vs. security-incident detection) and risked future detection rules misinterpreting routine admin changes as security signals. **Corrected:** added a dedicated, additive `PlatformAdminAuditEvent` model (migration `20260720180000_add_platform_admin_audit_event`, FK to `PlatformAdmin`, indexed by actor/time and action/time, create-only) and a narrow `platformAdminAudit.ts` service (`writePlatformAdminAuditEventInTx`, mirroring the existing `writeAuditLogInTx` pattern); the PATCH route now writes the `PlatformSetting` update and the audit row in a single Prisma transaction — proven atomic by a real FK-violation test (non-existent admin id forces the audit insert to fail), not a mock — and the `SecuritySignalEvent` write this feature had added is removed entirely. Updated tests in `platformAdmin.test.ts` (49/49 passing) cover: exactly-one-row-per-toggle in both directions with correct previous/new values and actor attribution, no patient data/secrets in the row, no success row on a rejected payload, no success row for an unauthorized caller, the atomic rollback behavior, and zero `SecuritySignalEvent` rows for this action. Also merged current `origin/main` (PR #185, `audit/kvkk-high-008-post-merge-readiness`, merge commit `4c439716d9d56a989a1beb177e426eaae65255dd`, documentation-only) into this branch via `git merge --no-ff origin/main`, resolving documentation-only conflicts in this file and `RISK_REGISTER.md` manually (both sides' content preserved, none deleted). See this task's delivery report for exact commands/exit codes/counts and the final tested commit SHA. **Capture-time note:** this correction pass itself did not merge PR #186, deploy, activate the flag, or touch production. **Subsequent repository fact:** PR #186 later merged into `main` as commit `1a82302861139441f07ff5c0209728d3e711728f`; deployment and production verification remain pending. R-061 remains `OPEN`.)

---

## 1. Program objective (Program hedefi)

NoraMedi; düzenlemeye tabi, çok kiracılı (multi-tenant) bir diş hekimliği ve sağlık operasyonları platformudur. Programın mimari hedefi:

- **Binlerce klinik tenant** ve kısa sürede yüzlerce klinik onboarding kapasitesi
- **Yüksek eşzamanlı kullanıcı aktivitesi** altında öngörülebilir performans
- **Güçlü tenant izolasyonu** (defense-in-depth: uygulama + veritabanı + altyapı katmanları)
- **KVKK ve sağlık sektörü mevzuat uyumu** (Türkiye)
- **T.C. Sağlık Bakanlığı ve diğer resmî entegrasyonlar** (adapter tabanlı platform)
- WhatsApp, Instagram, SMS, e-posta ve ses iletişim kanalları
- **Yaygın AI kullanımı**: AI Gateway, governance, maliyet/kullanım ölçümü, PII/PHI minimizasyonu
- **DICOM, CBCT, 2D/3D görüntüleme** ve klinik cihaz entegrasyonları; üçüncü taraf tıbbi görüntüleme AI entegrasyonları
- **Modüler monolit** mimari; yalnızca gerekçeli sınırlar için servis çıkarımı
- Yüksek erişilebilirlik, felaket kurtarma (DR), gözlemlenebilirlik ve kurumsal güvenlik
- Hızlı, etki-bazlı (affected) test mimarisi ile modüler geliştirme
- Feature flag ve entitlement ile kontrol edilen opsiyonel ticari modüller

## 2. Authoritative status rules (Yetkili durum kuralları)

### 2.1 Kaynak hiyerarşisi

1. Git commitleri, merge edilmiş PR'lar, deploy edilmiş revizyonlar, migration'lar ve test kanıtları
2. `docs/program/NORAMEDI_MASTER_TRACKER.md` (bu dosya)
3. İlgili faz dokümanı (`docs/program/phases/`)
4. Kabul edilmiş ADR'ler ([ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md](ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md))
5. Güncel depo kanıtı
6. Stratejik yol haritası dokümanları
7. Geçmiş konuşmalar ve ajan özetleri

Word yol haritası **stratejik referanstır**, canlı durum kaynağı değildir. Bir ajanın "completed" beyanı; işin incelendiğinin, merge edildiğinin, deploy edildiğinin veya production'da doğrulandığının kanıtı **değildir**.

### 2.2 Görev durum modeli

| Durum | Anlamı |
|---|---|
| `TODO` | Görev tanımlı, henüz başlamaya hazır değil veya sırada |
| `READY` | Ön koşulları sağlanmış, bir sonraki çalışmada başlatılabilir |
| `IN_PROGRESS` | Ajan üzerinde aktif çalışıyor |
| `AGENT_COMPLETED` | Ajan, istenen depo işinin bittiğine **inanıyor**; dış doğrulama yok |
| `REVIEW_REQUIRED` | Dış inceleyici (ChatGPT/kullanıcı) incelemeyi başlattı/istedi |
| `CHANGES_REQUESTED` | İnceleme sonucu düzeltme istendi; ajan yeniden çalışacak |
| `TESTS_PASSED` | İlgili test kapsamının geçtiği **dışarıdan teyit edildi** |
| `PR_OPEN` | Pull request açıldı, merge bekliyor |
| `MERGED` | Merge kanıtı (commit/PR referansı) **teyit edildi** |
| `DEPLOYED` | Deployment kanıtı (revizyon/ortam) **teyit edildi** |
| `PRODUCTION_VERIFIED` | Canlı smoke/kabul testi **başarıyla** yapıldı ve kaydedildi |
| `BLOCKED` | Dış bir engel nedeniyle ilerleyemiyor |
| `DEFERRED` | Bilinçli olarak ertelendi |
| `CANCELLED` | İptal edildi |

### 2.3 Ajan yetki sınırları

- Claude Code bir görevi en fazla `AGENT_COMPLETED` durumuna getirebilir.
- Claude Code şu durumları dış teyit olmadan **atayamaz**: `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, `TESTS_PASSED`, `MERGED`, `DEPLOYED`, `PRODUCTION_VERIFIED`.
- Claude Code `PR_OPEN` durumunu ancak **gerçek bir pull request açıldıktan sonra** ve PR referansıyla (numara/URL) kaydedebilir; PR açılmadan `PR_OPEN` yazılamaz.
- `MERGED` doğrulanmış merge kanıtı; `DEPLOYED` doğrulanmış deployment kanıtı; `PRODUCTION_VERIFIED` başarılı canlı smoke/kabul testi gerektirir.
- Kabul ve merge kararları dış incelemeye (ChatGPT/kullanıcı) aittir; ajan bu kararları veremez.

- **Failure-severity handling (governs how this task's findings must be read — do not read a failure here as automatically requiring, or automatically not requiring, production rollback):** A failed disposable-environment verification (rollback rehearsal or tenant-isolation test) does not, by itself, automatically execute or mandate an immediate production rollback. It DOES block: controlled-pilot approval, communication-consent enforcement activation, reconciliation activation, production backfill, freeze-boundary condition 4 closure, and any external "KVKK baseline stable" declaration. If the failure demonstrates an actual cross-clinic access issue, stale-default-clinic write attribution, patient-data integrity risk, or unsafe/non-reversible migration behavior, production impact must be assessed immediately and may require a hotfix, disabling the affected workflow where technically possible, an operational restriction, or a production rollback — chosen according to severity and rollback safety, not automatically. Do not claim all test failures automatically require rollback; do not claim they are automatically non-blocking.
- **Evidence required:** Rollback-rehearsal transcript/output against a disposable Postgres instance; multi-clinic tenant-isolation test transcript/output against a disposable, non-production database; independently-executed (not author-only) test-suite run output for both KVKK-HIGH-007 and KVKK-HIGH-008; a recorded R-061 accept/reject decision.
- **Execution relationship:** May run in parallel with KVKK-HIGH-006 (Direct Default-Clinic Scope Remediation — see `docs/compliance/KVKK_COMPLIANCE_AUDIT_AND_REMEDIATION.md`) only in a separate branch/worktree — F0-011-P2 is verification/documentation/test-evidence only, KVKK-HIGH-006 changes runtime route scoping; their diffs must not be combined into one PR. F0-012 (Controlled Pilot and General Launch Gate Definition) may also proceed in parallel as documentation-only gate-definition work, and must list both F0-011-P2 completion and KVKK-HIGH-006 remediation as controlled-pilot gate criteria unless later evidence explicitly changes that decision.
- **Blocking conditions:** Requires a disposable/local database and environment — no production or shared-database access. External review of the resulting rollback/tenant-impact evidence remains a separate step per §2.3.

## 8. Blocked tasks (Bloklu işler)

Aktif KVKK/gizlilik geliştirmesi dışarıdan teyit edilmiş kararlı bir taban çizgisine ulaşana kadar aşağıdaki **fiziksel mimari değişiklikleri BLOKLUDUR**:

1. Geniş Prisma şema değişiklikleri
2. Tenant `organizationId` backfill'leri
3. RLS migration'ları
4. Prisma tenant extension rollout'u
5. Fiziksel modül refactoring'i
6. Privacy modeli taşıma
7. Consent modeli taşıma
8. Retention modeli taşıma
9. Anonimleştirme iş akışı yeniden yapılandırması
10. Attachment fiziksel-silme iş akışı değişiklikleri
11. Storage-key migrasyonu
12. Geniş authentication middleware yeniden yapılandırması

F0 kapsamında **serbest** olan işler: dokümantasyon, depo envanteri, modül haritalama, test envanteri, ADR çalışması ve invaziv olmayan analiz.

7. **(Added 2026-07-20, post-deployment reconciliation pass; corrected within the same pass — see item 8)** KVKK-HIGH-008 is now deployed to production (migration applied, table/enum present, 0 correction rows, flags disabled — see top-of-file update) but [KVKK_ARCHITECTURE_FREEZE_BOUNDARY.md](KVKK_ARCHITECTURE_FREEZE_BOUNDARY.md) §5 conditions 4-5 remain unsatisfied for both the KVKK-HIGH-007 and KVKK-HIGH-008 migrations: no independently-evidenced rollback/tenant-impact verification exists for either, and no external "KVKK baseline stable" declaration has been made. A dedicated task to independently execute KVKK-HIGH-007/HIGH-008's test suites (currently author-reported only, not CI-covered — R-049) and to perform/evidence a rollback and tenant-impact check is the concrete blocker to closing condition 4. Separately, KVKK-HIGH-008's no-feature-flag design point (R-061) still requires an explicit human accept/reject decision now that the workflow is live in production with no kill switch. Of the previously-candidate Phase-0 remediation items: KVKK-HIGH-001 remains `Waiting for operational/infrastructure evidence`, KVKK-HIGH-003 remains `Waiting for legal review`, KVKK-HIGH-005 is confirmed still `N/A` (no active marketing-send path found in this pass's code review) — **KVKK-HIGH-006 is corrected below (item 8) from "already resolved" to `STILL_OPEN`, and is itself a ready, unblocked, new Phase-0 remediation code item.**
8. **(Added 2026-07-20, same reconciliation pass, evidence-tightening correction)** Item 7's original text (and the compliance document's KVKK-HIGH-006 row) claimed "zero live runtime `req.user.clinicId` usages remain," based on a grep for only the literal string `req.user.clinicId` (29 matches/11 files, all comments/tests). **That claim was wrong and is corrected here before commit, not merely appended.** A broader, reproducible search for the equivalent pattern `req\.user(!|\?)?\.clinicId` (i.e. including the codebase's dominant TypeScript non-null-assertion idiom `req.user!.clinicId`) across `server/src` finds 93 occurrences across 23 files. Of these, **47 are live runtime authorization/data-scope usages across 10 files** — `services.ts` (8), `postTreatment.ts` (7), `messages.ts` (7), `insuranceProvisions.ts` (6), `inventory.ts` (5), `paymentPlans.ts` (4), `dentalChart.ts` (3), `appointmentRequests.ts` (3), `reports.ts` (1, at `reports.ts:405`, the file's only route with zero scope validation of any kind), `middleware/planLimits.ts` (2) — each using `req.user!.clinicId` directly as the authorization/scope value for a Prisma or raw-SQL query, without the codebase's own established validated-scope helper (`validateAndGetClinicIdScope`/`validateAndGetScope`, `server/src/utils/clinicScope.ts:87,210`), which 6 of these 10 files already import and use correctly on *other* routes in the same file (an inconsistently-applied fix, not an absent one). No cross-organization leak was found (every usage stays within the requester's own `organizationId`, and AND-filtered lookups fail closed as 404); the concrete risk is multi-branch-admin functional denial plus a data-mis-attribution risk on writes (dental chart entries, insurance provisions, message templates, and — at `appointmentRequests.ts:192` — newly-created Patient/Appointment records stamped with an unvalidated default clinicId). **KVKK-HIGH-006 status: `STILL_OPEN`**, not resolved and not a documentation-closure task; its original acceptance criteria (fix only confirmed-incorrect usages) remain unmet. `docs/compliance/KVKK_COMPLIANCE_AUDIT_AND_REMEDIATION.md`'s KVKK-HIGH-006 row and 2026-07-20 change-history entry are corrected accordingly (in the same uncommitted pass, not appended, since neither had been committed). This is the corrected next-task input for §13 below.

9. **(Added 2026-07-21, docs-only reconciliation, branch `fix/kvkk-high006-default-clinic-scope`, base `origin/main` @ `7174f3bb3518bca6b2640694ee2f9264c293318f`, no drift.)** A separate, narrower question — whether the nullable `User.defaultClinicId` UI/navigation-preference field is ever used *directly* as an authorization or tenant-scope source — was investigated under this same KVKK-HIGH-006 task ID by mistake, across `usersImport.ts`, `instagramInbox.ts`, `whatsappInbox.ts`, `instagramClinicResolver.ts`, `relationGuards.ts`, `reminders.ts#getSystemUserForClinic`, and `taskAssignmentNotifier.ts`. No such usage was found (0 ambiguous paths, 0 violations). **This is reconciled here as a distinct, subordinate task, KVKK-HIGH-006-S1** (`VERIFIED_NO_VIOLATION_FOUND`; [evidence/KVKK-HIGH-006-S1_DEFAULT_CLINIC_ID_AUTHORIZATION_PATH_VERIFICATION.md](evidence/KVKK-HIGH-006-S1_DEFAULT_CLINIC_ID_AUTHORIZATION_PATH_VERIFICATION.md)) — **it does not replace, satisfy, supersede, close, mitigate, or reduce the required scope of KVKK-HIGH-006 above, which remains `STILL_OPEN` / `READY_FOR_DETAILED_SCOPING`.** `defaultClinicId` and `req.user.clinicId` are distinct: the former is a nullable display preference; the latter is the authenticated request's server-resolved clinic context (itself partly informed by `defaultClinicId` at login time, then validated against `allowedClinicIds`/`canAccessAllClinics`/clinic-existence/organization-match before being attached to `req.user` — never re-read as a raw per-request value). KVKK-HIGH-006 itself is about **inconsistent direct use of `req.user.clinicId`** in place of the centralized `validateAndGetClinicIdScope`/`validateAndGetScope` contract — a different and broader question than S1's.

   **Wording correction (same reconciliation):** item 8's phrasing "zero scope validation of any kind" (`reports.ts:405`) and its "unvalidated default clinicId" framing (`appointmentRequests.ts:192`) are preserved above as historical text, but should be read precisely: neither route has been shown to allow attacker-controlled or cross-organization access, because `req.user.clinicId` is never client-supplied per request. The confirmed, current-evidence risk shape for both is **OWNER/ORG_ADMIN branch-selection/access-completeness narrowing** (a multi-branch admin cannot target another accessible clinic through these specific endpoints) **plus inconsistent centralized-scope-contract usage on PHI-bearing routes** — not a demonstrated cross-tenant leak or attacker-controlled write. Both routes remain high-priority remediation candidates for that reason (PHI/reporting operations bypassing the accepted centralized contract), independent of the leak/no-leak distinction.

   **Count-discrepancy note:** an independent re-search of `req\.user(!\|\?)?\.clinicId` (excluding test files) in this reconciliation found 64 non-test matches, including in three files not individually named in item 8's 10-file list (`dashboard.ts`, `organizationBranches.ts`, `organizationWhatsApp.ts`). These three are, in fact, already accounted for in `docs/compliance/KVKK_COMPLIANCE_AUDIT_AND_REMEDIATION.md`'s "10 safe non-authorization usages" bucket (activity-log attribution / display-locale formatting), so this is **not** a scope gap in the original classification — it is confirmed consistent once the same file-level buckets are applied. This reconciliation did not itself reproduce the full 93-occurrence bucket-by-bucket classification line-by-line; **do not treat 64 as a corrected count, and do not treat 93/47 as freshly, independently re-verified without qualification** — a full occurrence-by-occurrence reproduction remains **KVKK-HIGH-006-S2**'s job (see §13).

   **Special high-risk review, this reconciliation:** full enclosing-function reads of `reports.ts` (`/reports/no-show-analysis`, line 404–510) and `appointmentRequests.ts` (`POST /:id/convert`, line 191–342) confirm: (a) `reports.ts:405`'s three `$queryRaw` calls are Prisma tagged-template parameterized (no SQL-injection surface) and every one is scoped only by the caller's own already-resolved `clinicId`; (b) `appointmentRequests.ts:192`'s newly-created `Patient`'s `organizationId` is independently re-derived from `prisma.clinic.findUnique({ where: { id: clinicId } })`, never trusted from request input, so even PHI-record creation cannot cross an organization boundary. Neither finding changes KVKK-HIGH-006's `STILL_OPEN` status or its priority ranking — both routes still bypass the accepted centralized scope contract and remain scheduled for remediation.

   **Separate, non-security backlog item identified (not implemented, not part of KVKK-HIGH-006):** notification/audit-attribution lookup completeness for `canAccessAllClinics` users in `reminders.ts#getSystemUserForClinic` and `taskAssignmentNotifier.ts` (a `canAccessAllClinics` user whose `defaultClinicId` differs from a given clinic, and who has no matching `userClinics` row, does not surface as a candidate system-user/notification-recipient for that clinic — a completeness gap, not a tenant-isolation defect). Recorded here for future backlog triage; not the next ordered task unless higher-priority work is exhausted.
